Dimensional Regularization of Bubble Diagrams in de Sitter Spacetime
Hongyu Zhang, [5mm] Department of Physics, Tsinghua University, Beijing 100084, China

Abstract
Correlators of large-scale fluctuations produced during cosmic inflation are major observables of inflationary cosmology. In cosmological collider physics, many interesting correlators are generated through loop processes. However, ultraviolet divergences often appear when computing the loop correlators, and a regularization is required. In this work, Källén-Lehmann representation and dimensional regularization are used to analytically compute various correlators with a bubble loop of massive bulk propagators in de Sitter spacetime. Examples include 4-point and 2-point correlators with 1-loop bubbe exchanges of derivatively coupled massive scalars or massive spin-1 bosons.



1  Introduction

It is widely believed that the universe has experienced a nearly exponential expansion in an extremely early epoch, known as inflation. The Hubble parameter
H
is nearly a constant in the inflationary spacetime. The spacetime of inflation is close to the Poincaré patch of
3+1
dimensional de Sitter spacetime
\text{dS}_{4}
, which is one of the maximally symmetric spacetimes. The Hubble parameter is the inverse of the curvature radius of dS.

The equal-time correlation functions are important quantities during inflation. These functions can be computed theoretically. The study of processes of quantum field theory in the inflationary spacetime has gained considerable interest. On the other side, quantum processes during cosmic inflation generate large-scale inhomogeneities of the universe. The inhomogeneities can be studied from the measurements including the temperature and the polarizations of the cosmological microwave background
[
1
,
2
]
, the large-scale structure survey
[
3
]
, and the more futuristic 21cm tomography
[
4
,
5
]
. From the measurements, the correlation functions can be extracted. Therefore, the correlation functions serve as fundamental links between quantum field theory in inflation and observational data. In this work, the correlation functions are called inflation correlators. There have been many works in the study of the inflation correlators
[

,

,

,

,

There are also extensive studies on analytical computations of part and full results of the cosmological correlators. Some methods are concluded to derive the expressions, such as Mellin space representation
[

[

]
. These techniques make it possible to get the expressions of many tree-level correlators.

The study of correlators with one loop is useful. In many particle models, the leading CC signal appears at the 1-loop level rather than the tree level. In these models, massive particles are created and annihilated in pairs. The tree-level process of these models is simply absent. These include models with most particles of the Standard Model in the symmetric phase
[
86
,
87
,
96
,
102
,
103
,
104
]
, new physical particles such as heavy neutrinos
[
116
]
and Kaluza-Klein
[
105
]
gravitons. There are also some models
[
142
,
109
,
141
]
in which the signals of the 1-loop level are more enhanced than those at the tree level (correlators with more loops remain subdominant so that perturbation theory still works).

The calculation of 1-loop correlators is challenging, due to the time ordering in the computation, the complicated mode functions and the loop momentum integral. The full results of 1-loop correlators are fewer than those of correlators at tree level.

[
172
]
provides the 1-loop bubble correction to the 2-point function from scalar fields of arbitrary mass in Euclidean dS. The 4-point function with 1-loop exchange of conformal scalar in the position space is calculated in
[
17
]
. Correlators of 4-point and 3-point with one bubble loop of scalar are calculated in
[
19
]
by using spectral decomposition. The result of the correlators with one loop can also be derived by using dispersion
[
171
]
.

The UV divergence is expected to appear in the 1-loop correlators. Therefore, regularization scheme should be applied to the correlators. There are several works on regularization in de Sitter spacetime, such as
[
173
,
174
,
175
,
85
,
176
,
22
,
177
,
178
]
. These works mainly focus on correlators with internal lines of massless scalars.
[
179
]
deals with loops from a conformal (
m^{2}=2H^{2}
) scalar field and provides a full calculation including renormalization.
[
180
]
studies dimensional regularization for daisy loops. It is necessary to find out a way to regularize correlators with internal lines of arbitrary mass.

There are several regularization schemes in flat spacetime, such as cutoff regularization, dimensional regularization and Pauli-Villars regularization. In this work, the correlators with a bubble loop are computed by using Källén-Lehmann representation. The integral in the representation is hard to calculate if there is a cutoff in the integration. If one tries to finish the Pauli-Villars regularization, it is difficult to take the asymptotic expansions of the cutoff
\Lambda
and subtract the divergences for
\Lambda\rightarrow\infty
from the correlators. Therefore, dimensional regularization is used in de Sitter spacetime in this work.

Dimensional regularization, introduced by ’t Hooft and Veltman in 1972
[
181
]
, is a widely used method for regulating divergent integrals. The advantage of dimensional regularization is that gauge invariance is maintained. In this regularization, the dimension of the spacetime is continued from 4 to an arbitrary number
D
. After the expressions of correlation functions and
S
-matrix elements are calculated in
D
dimensions, the limit
D\rightarrow 4
is taken. Then the divergences that occur when
D\rightarrow 4
are canceled by MS subtraction or
\overline{\text{MS}}
subtraction.

The difficulty is how to compute the divergence of
D\rightarrow 4
. The expressions of the 1-loop correlators are usually composed of divergent series. Each term of the series converges when
D\rightarrow 4
. Therefore, to take the divergence of the series, the properties of the series need to be clear, rather than each term.

The dimensional regularization of 1-loop 4-point and 3-point correlators with directly coupled massive scalars is finished in
[
19
]
. In this work, the dimensional regularization of 1-loop 4-point correlators with directly coupled massive scalars is done in another way. The expressions of the divergent series are regularized with the help of Riemann zeta functions. The asymptotic expansions of each term of the series regarding the summation index are used to subtract the divergences of the series. The sums of asymptotic expansions can be summed into sums of several Riemann zeta functions. After taking the series expansions of the Riemann zeta function with respect to
(D-4)
, the divergences of the correlators can be found. The method is also applied to get the analytical expressions of 1-loop 2-point correlators, 1-loop 4-point correlators with derivatively coupled massive scalars, and 1-loop correlators with a massive vector loop.

Outline of this work

The rest of the paper is organized as follows. In Sec.
2
, the loop seed integral defined in
[
19
]
and the spectral decomposition of the seed integral are reviewed. The expression of the loop seed integral is computed with some improvement to obtain a simpler result. Then the divergence that appears when the spatial dimension
d\rightarrow 3
is studied to make it possible to take dimensional regularization. The MS subtraction is applied to the seed integral after dimensional regularization.

In Sec.
3
, dimensional regularization is used to take the expressions of more difficult cases, such as 1-loop 2-point correlator, 1-loop correlator with derivative coupling, and 1-loop correlator with massive vector loop. The expressions of these models in flat spacetime are also calculated, and the coefficients of the divergences are compared with those in de Sitter spacetime. The conclusions and outlooks are given in Sec.
4
.

There are several appendices in this work. App.
A
collects some useful functions that are used in this work. The pole structure of the tree seed integral is studied in App.
B
. This study can simplify the calculation of the spectral integral in this work. App.
C
provides the details of finding the expression of the 2-point correlator at tree level. The expression of the 2-point correlator with one loop is obtained by integrating the spectral integral of the correlator at tree level. The flat spacetime limit of two models introduced in Sec.
3
is computed in App.
D
. The coefficient of the divergences for
D\rightarrow 4(d\rightarrow 3)
are taken to compare with those obtained in de Sitter spacetime. It is expected that the coefficients of the divergences should agree with those in flat spacetime if
H\rightarrow 0
. In App.
E
, the spectral functions of Källén-Lehmann representation of the two models in this work are calculated. Embedding formalism is introduced in this appendix to finish the calculation. App.
F
collects some expressions which are omitted in the main text.
[
172
]
and
[
23
]
give two different kinds of spectral functions to calculate the correlators with one bubble loop. App.
G
studies the relation between the two kinds of spectral functions. App.
H
proves the convergence of the series of 1-loop 2-point correlators.

Notations and conventions

In this work, the metric is
\mathrm{d}s^{2}=a^{2}(\tau)(-\mathrm{d}\tau^{2}+\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}^{2})
where
\tau\in(-\infty,0)
is the conformal time,
\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is the conformal coordinate. In the Poincaré patch of the dS spacetime, the scale factor
a(\tau)=-1/(H\tau)
. In this work, the Hubble parameter
H
is set to 1.

The external spatial momenta of a 4-point correlator are labeled as
\mathbf{k}_{i}\ (i=1,2,3)
. Their magnitudes are
k_{i}\equiv|\mathbf{k}_{i}|
. The
s
-channel momentum is defined by
\mathbf{k}_{s}\equiv\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2}
. The shorthand notations for sums of several indexed quantities are used in this work. For example,

\displaystyle k_{ij}\equiv k_{i}+k_{j},

\displaystyle n_{12}\equiv n_{1}+n_{2},

\displaystyle p_{12}\equiv p_{1}+p_{2}.

(1)

The shorthand
\bar{p}_{12}\equiv p_{1}-p_{2}
is also used. The momentum ratios are
r_{1}\equiv k_{s}/k_{12},r_{2}\equiv k_{s}/k_{34}
.

2  Dimensional Regularization of the Basic Bubble Diagram

Consider the 1-loop correlator shown in Fig.
1
.

Figure 1:
Feynman diagram of 1-loop 4-point correlator with a scalar loop.

The diagrammatic notations are introduced in
[
93
]
. The external legs are bulk-to-boundary propagators of a conformal scalar field
\phi_{c}
, i.e., a scalar field with mass
m_{c}^{2}=(d^{2}-1)/4
. The two internal lines represent the bulk propagators of a real scalar field
\sigma
of mass
m
. It is convenient to introduce the mass parameter
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu=\sqrt{m^{2}-d^{2}/4}
. Suppose the interaction is

\Delta\mathscr{L}=-\frac{1}{4}a^{d+1}\phi_{c}^{2}\sigma^{2}.

(2)

The model with the above interaction is called
\sigma\sigma
model in this work. The bulk-to-boundary propagator
C_{\mathsf{a}}(k;\tau)
of the conformal scalar field reads:

C_{\mathsf{a}}(k;\tau)=\frac{(\tau\tau_{f})^{(d-1)/2}}{2k}e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}k\tau}.

(3)

Here
\tau_{f}
is a final-time cutoff, and
\mathsf{a}=\pm
is the Schwinger-Keldysh (SK) index of the bulk point. The expression of the bulk-propagator
D_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
is

\displaystyle D_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\pm\pm}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=

\displaystyle~D_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\gtrless}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\theta(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})+D_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\lessgtr}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\theta(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}),

(4)

\displaystyle D_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\pm\mp}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=

\displaystyle~D_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\lessgtr}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2}).

(5)

Here two “homogeneous” propagators are introduced,

\displaystyle D_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,>}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=\displaystyle\frac{\,{\pi}\,}{4}e^{-\pi(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{*})/2}(\tau_{1}\tau_{2})^{d/2}\text{H}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{(1)}(-k\tau_{1})\text{H}_{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{*}}^{(2)}(-k\tau_{2}),

(6)

and,
D_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,<}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=D_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,>}^{*}(k;\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
.

With the expressions of bulk-to-boundary and bulk propagators given above, the expression of
s
-channel of the 1-loop 4-point function is built with the SK formalism
[
173
,
93
]
. It can be written as:

\langle\phi_{c,\mathbf{k}_{1}}\phi_{c,\mathbf{k}_{2}}\phi_{c,\mathbf{k}_{3}}\phi_{c,\mathbf{k}_{4}}\rangle_{s}=(2\pi)^{d}\delta^{(d)}(\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{k}_{3}+\mathbf{k}_{4})\mathcal{L}_{\phi_{c},\sigma\sigma}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2},\mathbf{k}_{3},\mathbf{k}_{4}),

(7)

where the loop amplitude
\mathcal{L}_{\phi_{c},\sigma\sigma}
is

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\phi_{c},\sigma\sigma}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b}=\pm}\mathsf{ab}\int_{-\infty}^{\tau_{f}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\tau_{1}}{(-\tau_{1})^{d+1}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\tau_{2}}{(-\tau_{2})^{d+1}}

\displaystyle\times C_{\mathsf{a}}(k_{1},\tau_{1})C_{\mathsf{a}}(k_{2},\tau_{1})C_{\mathsf{b}}(k_{3},\tau_{2})C_{\mathsf{b}}(k_{4},\tau_{2})\mathcal{Q}_{\sigma\sigma,\mathsf{ab}}\big(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\big).

(8)

Here the loop momentum integral
\mathcal{Q}_{\sigma\sigma,\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mathsf{ab}}
is defined as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\sigma\sigma,\mathsf{ab}}\big(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\big)\equiv

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{d}\mathbf{q}}{(2\pi)^{d}}D_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mathsf{ab}}\big(q;\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\big)D_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,\mathsf{ab}}\big(|\mathbf{k}_{s}-\mathbf{q}|;\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\big).

(9)

It is found that many massive 1-loop correlators have similar structures. They can be generated from the loop seed integral
[
19
]
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{p_{1}p_{2}}_{\sigma\sigma}(r_{1},r_{2})\equiv-\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{2}\!\sum_{\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b}=\pm}\!\!\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}\,k_{s}^{d+2+p_{12}}\!\int_{-\infty}^{0}\!{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{1}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{2}(-\tau_{1})^{p_{1}}(-\tau_{2})^{p_{2}}e^{\mathrm{i}\mathsf{a}k_{12}\tau_{1}+\mathrm{i}\mathsf{b}k_{34}\tau_{2}}\mathcal{Q}_{\sigma\sigma,\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}\big(k_{s};\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\big).

(10)

The factor
k_{s}^{d+2+p_{12}}
is inserted to make the integral dimensionless. It can be seen that the loop seed integral depends on various external momenta only through two ratios
r_{1}\equiv k_{s}/k_{12}
and
r_{2}\equiv k_{s}/k_{34}
. The seed integral can be generated by the following interaction:

\Delta\mathscr{L}=-\frac{1}{4}a^{d-1-p_{1}}\phi_{c}^{2}\sigma^{2}-\frac{1}{4}a^{d-1-p_{2}}\phi_{c}^{2}\sigma^{2}.

(11)

The amplitude
\mathcal{L}_{\phi_{c},\sigma\sigma}
defined as (
2
) can be expressed as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\phi_{c},\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}=\displaystyle\frac{\,{(-\tau_{f})^{2(d-1)}}\,}{16k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4}k_{s}^{d-2}}\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma}^{-2,-2}(r_{1},r_{2}).

(12)

In the rest of this section, the expression of the loop seed integral is listed and the divergence of
d\rightarrow 3
is studied.

2.1
Expression of the Seed Integral

The loop in the seed integral can be written in terms of the Källén-Lehmann integration of the propagator of a free scalar field
[
23
]
. Therefore, the correlator with a bubble can be derived from the integration of the correlator of tree level introduced in
[
151
]
:

\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime}\rho_{\sigma\sigma}^{\mathcal{P},0}(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime})\mathcal{I}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2}).

(13)

It is convenient to break the tree seed integral into three pieces according to their analytic properties at
r_{1,2}=0
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{p_{1}p_{2}}_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}(r_{1},r_{2})=\mathcal{I}_{\text{NL},\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+\mathcal{I}_{\text{L},\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+\mathcal{I}_{\text{BG},\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2}).

(14)

The three terms are the nonlocal-signal piece (NL), the local signal piece (L), and the background piece (BG). The expressions for the three pieces when
r_{1}<r_{2}
are given below
[
151
,
19
]
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\text{NL},\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,d}^{p_{1}p_{2}}\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,d}^{p_{1}}(r_{1})\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,d}^{p_{2}}(r_{2})(r_{1}r_{2})^{+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow(-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)),

(15)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\text{L},\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle-\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,d}^{p_{1}p_{2}}\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,d}^{p_{1}}(r_{1})\mathbf{F}_{-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,d}^{p_{2}}(r_{2})\Big(\frac{r_{1}}{r_{2}}\Big)^{+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow(-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)),

(16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\text{BG},\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell,j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{\ell+1}\sin[\frac{\pi}{2}(p_{12}+d)](\ell+1)_{2j+d+p_{12}+1}}{2^{2j+1}\big(\tfrac{\ell-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+p_{2}+1}{2}+\tfrac{d}{4}\big)_{j+1}\big(\tfrac{\ell+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+p_{2}+1}{2}+\tfrac{d}{4}\big)_{j+1}}r_{1}^{2j+d+p_{12}+2}\Big(\frac{r_{1}}{r_{2}}\Big)^{\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{\ell,j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{\ell+1}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,d}^{p_{1}p_{2}}\sin(\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)(\ell+1)_{2j+d+p_{12}+1}}{2^{2j-1}\big(\tfrac{\ell-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+p_{2}+1}{2}+\tfrac{d}{4}\big)_{j+1}\big(\tfrac{\ell+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+p_{2}+1}{2}+\tfrac{d}{4}\big)_{j+1}}r_{1}^{2j+d+p_{12}+2}\Big(\frac{r_{1}}{r_{2}}\Big)^{\ell}+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow(-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)),

(17)

where the coefficient
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,d}^{p_{1}p_{2}}
and the function
\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,d}^{p}(r)
are defined by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,d}^{p_{1}p_{2}}\equiv

\displaystyle~\displaystyle\frac{\,{1}\,}{8}\csc^{2}(\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Big\{\cos\displaystyle\frac{\,{\pi\bar{p}_{12}}\,}{2}+\cos\Big[\pi\Big(\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+\displaystyle\frac{\,{p_{12}+d}\,}{2}\Big)\Big]\Big\},

(18)

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu,d}^{p}(r)\equiv

\displaystyle~(2r)^{p+d/2+1}\times{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}\left[\begin{matrix}\tfrac{d}{4}+\tfrac{1}{2}+\tfrac{p}{2}+\tfrac{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{2},\tfrac{d}{4}+1+\tfrac{p}{2}+\tfrac{\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}{2}\\
1+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\end{matrix}\middle|r^{2}\right].

(19)

The shorthands
p_{12}\equiv p_{1}+p_{2}
and
\bar{p}_{12}\equiv p_{1}-p_{2}
are introduced. The function
{}_{2}\mathcal{F}_{1}
is the dressed hypergeometric function defined in (
162
).

The spectral function
\rho_{\sigma\sigma}^{\mathcal{P},0}(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime})
of Källén-Lehmann representation is
[
23
]

\rho_{\sigma\sigma}^{\mathcal{P},0}(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime})=\frac{\Gamma\big(\frac{d+1}{2}\big)\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime}\sinh(\pi\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime})}{2^{6-d}\pi^{\frac{d+7}{2}}\Gamma(d)\Gamma\big(\frac{d}{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime}\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{d}{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime}\big)}\prod_{\pm,\pm,\pm}\Gamma\bigg(\frac{\frac{d}{2}\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\pm\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime}}{2}\bigg).

(20)

The expression above is quite different from that labeled as
\rho_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{\text{dS}}(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime})

]
. It can be shown that the relation between these two kinds of spectral functions is (App.

)

\rho_{\sigma\sigma}^{\mathcal{P},0}(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime})=\frac{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\big(\rho_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{\text{dS}}(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime})-\rho_{\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{\text{dS}}(-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime})\big).

(21)

For the case
0<r_{1}<r_{2}<1
, it can be derived that
0<r_{1}r_{2}<1
and
0<r_{1}/r_{2}<1
. Therefore, the integral contour in (
13
) should be closed with a large semi-circle in the lower-half
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime}
-plane for the term proportional to
(r_{1}r_{2})^{+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime}}
in
\mathcal{I}_{\text{NL},\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}
and the term proportional to
(r_{1}/r_{2})^{+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime}}
in
\mathcal{I}_{\text{L},\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}
. For the
(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow(-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu))
terms of the terms mentioned before, the integral contour should be closed with a large semi-circle in the upper-half
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime}
-plane. For the terms of the background
\mathcal{I}_{\text{BG},\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}
, the integral contour can be closed with a semi-circle in an arbitrary half
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime}
-plane. However, it is better to close the contour in the lower plane for the term shown in (
2.1
) directly while in the upper plane for the
(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow(-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu))
term. The reason is that there are no poles of the integrand from the sum of

term in
\mathcal{I}_{\text{NL},\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}
,

term in
\mathcal{I}_{\text{L},\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}^{p_{1}p_{2}}
and the term shown in (
2.1
) directly in the lower
\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu^{\prime}
-plane. This is right for the other three terms. The poles of the integrand of (

The full result of the loop seed integral can be divided into several pieces in terms of their analytic properties in the squeezed limit
r_{1,2}\rightarrow 0
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})=\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma,\text{NS}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma,\text{LS}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2})+\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma,\text{BG}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}(r_{1},r_{2}).

(25)

The results of nonlocal and local signals are:

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma,\text{NS}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(r_{1}r_{2})^{d/2+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\sin[\pi(\mbox{$\frac{\,{d}\,}{2}$}+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)]}{8\pi^{d/2}\Gamma\big(\tfrac{d}{2}\big)\sin^{2}(\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(1+n)_{\frac{d}{2}-1}\big[(1+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+n)_{\frac{d}{2}-1}\big]^{2}(1+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+n)_{\frac{d}{2}-1}}\,}{(1+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+2n)_{d-1}}

\displaystyle\times(\mbox{$\frac{\,{d}\,}{2}$}+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+2n)\mathcal{C}_{2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+d/2+2n,d}^{p_{1}p_{2}}\mathbf{F}_{2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+d/2+2n,d}^{p_{1}}(r_{1})\mathbf{F}_{2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+d/2+2n,d}^{p_{2}}(r_{2})(r_{1}r_{2})^{2n}

\displaystyle+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow(-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)),

(26)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma,\text{LS}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}=

\displaystyle\frac{(r_{1}/r_{2})^{d/2+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu}\sin[\pi(\mbox{$\frac{\,{d}\,}{2}$}+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)]}{8\pi^{d/2}\Gamma\big(\tfrac{d}{2}\big)\sin^{2}(\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{(1+n)_{\frac{d}{2}-1}\big[(1+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+n)_{\frac{d}{2}-1}\big]^{2}(1+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+n)_{\frac{d}{2}-1}}\,}{(1+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+2n)_{d-1}}

\displaystyle\times(\mbox{$\frac{\,{d}\,}{2}$}+2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+2n)\mathcal{C}_{2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+d/2+2n,d}^{p_{1}p_{2}}\mathbf{F}_{2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+d/2+2n,d}^{p_{1}}(r_{1})\mathbf{F}_{-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu-d/2-2n,d}^{p_{2}}(r_{2})\bigg(\frac{r_{1}}{r_{2}}\bigg)^{2n}

\displaystyle+(\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\rightarrow(-\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)).

(27)

They are from the integration of signals of the tree seed integral, while the poles are Set A and Set C.

The expression of the background consists of multiple parts:

\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma,\text{BG}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}=\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma,\text{(B)}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}+\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma,(\text{3A})}^{p_{1}p_{2}}+\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma,(\text{3B})}^{p_{1}p_{2}}+\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma,(\text{3C})}^{p_{1}p_{2}}.

\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma,\text{(B)}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}
comes from the Set B poles of the integrand of (
13
), while the signals of the integrand are considered. The expression is:

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma,\text{(B)}}^{p_{1}p_{2}}=

\displaystyle~\frac{\sin\big(\frac{\pi d}{2}\big)}{4\pi^{d/2}\Gamma\big(\tfrac{d}{2}\big)\sin^{2}(\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\displaystyle\frac{\,{\big[(1+n)_{\frac{d}{2}-1}\big]^{2}(1+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+n)_{\frac{d}{2}-1}(1-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+n)_{\frac{d}{2}-1}}\,}{(1+2n)_{d-1}}(\mbox{$\frac{\,{d}\,}{2}$}+2n)\mathcal{C}_{d/2+2n,d}^{p_{1}p_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\mathbf{F}_{d/2+2n,d}^{p_{1}}(r_{1})r_{1}^{2n+d/2}\Big[\mathbf{F}_{d/2+2n,d}^{p_{2}}(r_{2})r_{2}^{2n+d/2}+\mathbf{F}_{-d/2-2n,d}^{p_{2}}(r_{2})r_{2}^{-2n-d/2}\Big].

(29)

The pieces
\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma,(\text{3A})}^{p_{1}p_{2}}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma,(\text{3A})}^{p_{1}p_{2}}=

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\Bigg[\begin{matrix}\frac{d}{2}+n,\frac{d}{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+n,\frac{d}{2}-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+n,-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+n+\frac{1}{2}\\
\frac{d+1}{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+n,-2\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+n+1,-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+n+1\end{matrix}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\times r_{1}^{2j+d+p_{12}+2}\bigg(\frac{r_{1}}{r_{2}}\bigg)^{\ell},

(30)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\sigma\sigma,(\text{3B})}^{p_{1}p_{2}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell,j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{\ell}\big(\frac{d}{2}+2n\big)\mathcal{C}_{\frac{d}{2}+2n,d}^{p_{1}p_{2}}\sin^{2}\big(\frac{\pi d}{2}\big)(\ell+1)_{2j+d+p_{12}+1}}{2^{2j+d}\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}\sin^{2}(\pi\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu)\Gamma\big(\frac{d}{2}\big)n!\big(\frac{\ell+p_{2}+1}{2}-n\big)_{j+1}\big(\frac{\ell+d+p_{2}+1}{2}+n\big)_{j+1}}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma\Bigg[\begin{matrix}\frac{d}{2}+\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+n,\frac{d}{2}+n,\frac{d}{2}-\mathrm{i}\mkern 2.0mu\widetilde{\mkern-2.0mu\nu\mkern-2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu+n,n+\frac{1}{2}\\
